hold-door-trade

Schmidt does not define this compound. He lists it among the words forming the latter part in compositions, under Trade, and defines both halves. Holddoor: trade, the trade of a bawd or pimp. Trade: subst. 1) traffic, commerce.

Passage not yet satisfactorily explained: H. hour, that called your grace to breakfast once, R3 IV, 4, 175 (Nares: "the phrase of dining with duke

Humphrey

which is still current, originated in the following manner.

Humphrey

duke of Gloucester, though really buried at St. Alban's, was supposed to have a monument in old St. Paul's, from which one part of the church was termed Duke Humphrey's walk. In this, as the church was then a place of the most public resort, they who had no means of procuring a dinner, frequently loitered about, probably in hopes of meeting with an invitation , but under pretence of looking at the monuments.")
